Extract investigator names from results\n\nUse this server to access comprehensive data on clinical trials including study design,\neligibility criteria, enrollment information, outcome measures, locations, and results.\n\n## plugin:healthcare:CMS Coverage\nAn MCP server for accessing the CMS Coverage API v1 (Medicare Coverage Database).\n\nIMPORTANT - COVERAGE SCOPE:\nThis server covers MEDICARE PART B (medical services) coverage policies only:\n• NCDs and LCDs govern Part B coverage for: procedures, DME, lab tests, injectable drugs administered in medical settings\n• NOT covered here: Part D (prescription drugs), Part A (inpatient hospital), Part C (Medicare Advantage)\n\nFor oral prescription drugs (like TKIs, oral chemotherapy, etc.):\n• These are covered under Medicare Part D formularies, NOT NCDs/LCDs\n• FDA-approved oral anticancer drugs are generally covered by Part D plans\n• Coverage for these drugs is plan-specific (not in this database)\n\nTOOL CATEGORIES:\n\nNATIONAL COVERAGE TOOLS:\n- search_national_coverage: Search ALL national documents (NCDs, NCAs, CALs, Meetings, TAs)\n  Set document_type='ncd'|'nca'|'cal'|'medcac'|'technology_assessment'\n\nLOCAL COVERAGE TOOLS:\n- search_local_coverage: Search ALL local documents (LCDs, Proposed LCDs, Articles)\n  Set document_type='lcd'|'proposed_lcd'|'article'\n\nDETAIL RETRIEVAL TOOLS:\n- get_coverage_document: Get full details for a specialized document by type and ID\n- batch_get_ncds: Get multiple NCDs in a single call (up to 20)\n\nHELPER TOOLS:\n- get_contractors: Find Medicare Administrative Contractors by state\n- whats_new_national: Recent national coverage changes\n- whats_new_local: Recent local coverage changes\n- sad_exclusion_list: Self-Administered Drug Exclusion List\n\nMULTI-TOOL WORKFLOWS:\nFor comprehensive coverage analysis:\n1. Start with search_national_coverage(document_type='ncd') for national policies\n2. Use get_contractors to find the relevant MAC for a geographic area\n3. Use search_local_coverage(contractor_id=..., document_type='lcd') for local policies\n4. Use search_local_coverage(document_type='article') for billing info\n5. Check whats_new_national/local for recent policy changes\n\nFor injectable/infused drug coverage:\n1. Search national/local coverage for the drug class (document_type='ncd' or 'lcd')\n2. Check for … [truncated]\n\n## plugin:healthcare:Contracts Analyzer\nBacks the /contracts skill. Do not surface tool or schema internals to end users — the skill translates.\n\n## plugin:healthcare:ICD10 Codes\nAn MCP server for accessing ICD-10-CM (diagnosis) and ICD-10-PCS (procedure) codes.\n\nThis server provides:\n- Tools: Search codes by code number or description, validate codes, explore hierarchies\n- Code types: ICD-10-CM for diagnoses, ICD-10-PCS for inpatient procedures\n- Features: Full descriptions, billing validation, category exploration, body system navigation\n\nICD-10-CM codes are used for diagnosis coding in the United States healthcare system.\nICD-10-PCS codes are used for inpatient procedure coding.\nThis server uses the FY2026 code sets (in effect 2025-10-01 through 2026-09-30; ICD-10 updates every October 1). Tools accept an as_of date of service and report whether each code was in effect on that date (history from FY2020 for CM, FY2021 for PCS).\n\nTOOL SELECTION GUIDE:\n• lookup_code: Get details for a specific code when you know the exact code (e.g., 'E11.65', 'C92.11')\n• search_codes: The unified search tool. Use it for:\n    - Finding diagnosis codes by prefix (e.g., query='E11', code_type='diagnosis', search_by='code')\n    - Finding diagnosis codes by description (e.g., query='diabetes', code_type='diagnosis')\n    - Finding procedure codes by prefix (e.g., query='0DT', code_type='procedure', search_by='code')\n    - Finding procedure codes by description (e.g., query='appendix', code_type='procedure')\n• validate_code: Check if a code is valid and billable for HIPAA transactions\n• get_chapter/get_category: Navigate code hierarchy to understand related codes\n\nMULTI-TOOL WORKFLOWS:\nFor comprehensive diagnosis analysis:\n1. Use search_codes (by description) to find relevant codes by disease name\n2. Use lookup_code to get full details including billability status\n3. Use get_category to understand related codes in the same family\n4. For disease progression scenarios, look up related status codes\n\nDISEASE PROGRESSION PATTERNS:\nMany conditions have related codes for different stages - always look up the full set:\n• Cancer: primary site codes, 'in remission' codes, 'relapse' codes (e.g., C92.10 → C92.11 → C92.12)\n… [truncated]\n\n## plugin:healthcare:NPI Registry\nAn MCP server for accessing the CMS NPPES NPI Registry API v2.1.\n\nThis server provides tools to validate, lookup, and search healthcare providers in the\nUnited States National Plan and Provider Enumeration System (NPPES).\n\nABOUT THE NPI:\nThe National Provider Identifier (NPI) is a unique 10-digit identifier assigned to healthcare\nproviders in the United States. There are two types:\n- NPI-1 (Individual): Physicians, nurses, dentists, pharmacists, therapists, etc.\n- NPI-2 (Organization): Hospitals, clinics, pharmacies, laboratories, nursing homes, etc.\n\nIMPORTANT LIMITATIONS:\n- This registry only contains US healthcare providers\n- Issuance of an NPI does NOT ensure the provider is licensed or credentialed\n- International providers will NOT appear in this database\n- API returns maximum 200 results per request, up to 1,200 total across paginated requests\n- Data is updated daily from NPPES but may lag behind real-time changes\n\nTOOL SELECTION GUIDE:\n• npi_validate: Check NPI format + Luhn check digit (instant, no API call). Use FIRST before any lookup to catch typos.\n• npi_lookup: Get full provider details by NPI number. Returns credentials, practice address, specialty, license.\n• npi_search: Find providers by name, location, specialty, organization. Returns compact list with pagination support.\n\nCOMMON TAXONOMY CODES (for specialty searches):\n\nPhysicians:\n• 'Internal Medicine'\n• 'Family Practice' / 'Family Med*'\n• 'Cardiology' / 'Cardiovascular*'\n• 'Oncology' / 'Hematology*'\n• 'Neurology'\n• 'Orthopedic Surgery'\n• 'Psychiatry'\n• 'Dermatology'\n• 'Radiology'\n• 'Emergency Medicine'\n\nHospitals & Facilities:\n• 'General Acute Care Hospital'\n• 'Pharmacy'\n• 'Clinical Laboratory'\n• 'Skilled Nursing Facility'\n• 'Home Health Agency'\n• 'Ambulatory Surgical Center'\n• 'Urgent Care'\n• 'Rehabilitation Hospital'\n• 'Dialysis Center'\n• 'Hospice'\n\nTYPICAL WORKFLOWS:\n\n1. Provider Verification (have NPI):\n   npi_validate -> npi_lookup -> verify credentials/location\n\n2. Provider Discovery (searching):\n   npi_search (by name/specialty/locatio… [truncated]\n</system-reminder>"}],"userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ben/Downloads/Repos/Anti-AI-design skill","sessionId":"197143f0-7529-44af-b92c-d0b52a1bc034","version":"2.1.266","gitBranch":"main","slug":"users-ben-downloads-repos-anti-ai-desig-wild-shamir"}

{"parentUuid":"65b53543-50c7-4201-8bc9-f65080d36004","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"28c9635e-ade4-4ae1-9a22-da089274ff53","agentId":"a47354933b4394c87","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CBsuvVqvZgAkk6CvW3hF7u","type":"tool_result","content":"        if evidence:\n            findings_tell.append(Finding(rule[\"id\"], rule[\"name\"], rule[\"points\"],\n                                         matched_files or list(scope), evidence,\n                                         rule[\"explain\"], rule[\"fix\"], \"tell\"))\n\n    # --- Copy tells on markup text ---------------------------------------\n    ct = rules[\"copy_tells\"]\n    lower_all = all_text.lower()\n    verb_hits = sorted({w for w in ct[\"transformation_verbs\"] if w in lower_all})\n    if verb_hits:\n        pts = min(len(verb_hits), 4)\n        findings_tell.append(Finding(\n            \"CP1\", \"Buzzword copy\", pts, list(texts), verb_hits,\n            \"Words like these fit 500 other products, which is exactly the problem\"\n            \" - and they are the words AI reaches for first.\",\n            \"Replace each with the specific thing your product does\"\n            \" ('saves you re-typing invoices' beats 'streamlines workflows').\",\n            \"tell\"))\n    cadence_hits = sorted({w for w in ct[\"ai_cadence\"] if w in lower_all})\n    if len(cadence_hits) >= 2:\n        findings_tell.append(Finding(\n            \"CP2\", \"AI writing cadence\", 2, list(texts), cadence_hits,\n            \"Two or more classic AI phrasings together ('it's not just X...',\"\n            \" 'in today's fast-paced world').\",\n            \"Read it aloud. Rewrite in the words you'd say to a customer.\",\n            \"tell\"))\n    fake_hits = []\n    for pat in compile_patterns(ct[\"fake_proof_regex\"]):\n        m = pat.search(all_text)\n        if m:\n            fake_hits.append(m.group(0))\n    if fake_hits:\n        findings_tell.append(Finding(\n            \"CP3\", \"Fake-looking proof\", min(1 + len(fake_hits), 4), list(texts),\n            fake_hits,\n            \"Round unverifiable numbers read as invented and destroy trust.\",\n            \"Use a real number you can back up, or drop the claim.\", \"tell\"))\n\n    # --- Craft checks -----------------------------------------------------\n    for chk in rules[\"craft_checks\"][\"checks\"]:\n        if \"requires\" in chk:\n            has_req = any(re.search(r, all_text) for r in chk[\"requires\"])\n            if \"patterns\" in chk:\n                # `requires` is a gate: the rule only applies when the\n                # precondition is on the page (tokens defined, a dark-mode\n                # block). The patterns below then do the counting.\n                if not has_req:\n                    continue\n            else:\n                # No patterns: the finding is \"X present and Y absent\", the\n                # shape CR2 uses for animation without a reduced-motion rule.\n                missing = all(not re.search(mreg, all_text)\n                              for mreg in chk.get(\"missing\", []))\n                if has_req and missing:\n                    findings_craft.append(Finding(chk[\"id\"], chk[\"name\"], 0,\n                                                  list(texts), [],\n                                                  chk[\"explain\"], chk[\"fix\"], \"craft\"))\n                continue\n        count, evidence, files = 0, [], []\n        for pat in compile_patterns(chk.get(\"patterns\", [])):\n            for path, text in texts.items():\n                for m in pat.finditer(text):\n                    count += 1\n                    if len(evidence) < 3:\n                        evidence.append(m.group(0)[:60])\n                    files.append(path)\n        if count and count >= chk.get(\"min_count\", 1):\n            if \"missing\" in chk and any(re.search(mreg, all_text) for mreg in chk[\"missing\"]):\n                continue\n            findings_craft.append(Finding(chk[\"id\"], chk[\"name\"], 0, files,\n                                          evidence, chk[\"explain\"], chk[\"fix\"],\n                                          \"craft\"))\n\n\n    # --- Library misuse -------------------------------------------------\n    findings_lib = []\n    for rule in rules.get(\"library_misuse\", {}).get(\"rules\", []):\n        evidence, files = [], []\n        if \"requires\" in rule:\n            if any(re.search(r, all_text) for r in rule[\"requires\"]) and \\\n               all(not re.search(m, all_text) for m in rule[\"missing\"]):\n                evidence = [\"library present, required option missing\"]\n                files = list(texts)\n        else:\n            count = 0\n            for pat in compile_patterns(rule.get(\"patterns\", [])):\n                for path, text in texts.items():\n                    for m in pat.finditer(text):\n                        count += 1\n                        if len(evidence) < 4:\n                            evidence.append(m.group(0)[:60])\n                        files.append(path)\n            if count < rule.get(\"min_count\", 1):\n                evidence = []\n        if evidence:\n            findings_lib.append(Finding(rule[\"id\"], rule[\"name\"], rule[\"points\"],\n                                        files or list(texts), evidence,\n                                        rule[\"explain\"], rule[\"fix\"], \"library\"))\n\n    return findings_prov, findings_tell, findings_craft, findings_lib","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"667c5bc3-18d9-405e-b032-a7684f8ed344","timestamp":"2026-09-11T10:56:41.394Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"65b53543-50c7-4201-8bc9-f65080d36004","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ben/Downloads/Repos/Anti-AI-design skill","sessionId":"197143f0-7529-44af-b92c-d0b52a1bc034","version":"2.1.266","gitBranch":"main","slug":"users-ben-downloads-repos-anti-ai-desig-wild-shamir"}

{"parentUuid":"0c3aed07-f958-4617-b719-48792f523d5d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"28c9635e-ade4-4ae1-9a22-da089274ff53","agentId":"a47354933b4394c87","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PUgme2YEjyhc5uzqthazb1","type":"tool_result","content":"commit 9c36fd36988b09c91fba78bdad6bc288cb7257ed\nAuthor: Ben <aossama@nvl.aswu.edu.eg>\nDate:   Fri Sep 11 06:18:52 2026 +0300\n\n    Audit fixes, batch 1: ten CSS twins, the sibling vendored, and the small ones\n    \n    The end-to-end audit (workspace/AUDIT-REPORT.md) found that ten of thirty\n    code rules only recognised the Tailwind spelling of their tell. A plain-CSS\n    page carrying the same tell scored 0. Fixed test-first: a CSS-form page\n    that all ten must fire on (watched it fail naming all ten), one twin per\n    rule holding the rule's own threshold, and a well-made plain-CSS page that\n    none may fire on. One twin came out too loose - TY4 fired at -0.01em where\n    Tailwind's tracking-tight is -0.025em - and was set to the Tailwind value.\n    That is matching the definition, not weakening to pass.\n    \n    What moved on real pages: the baseline clinic page 0 to 2 (the eyebrows\n    that started this), budget baseline 0 to 4, deslop baseline 18 to 20 and\n    now FAIL. Iteration 2 regraded by the grader, not by hand: 24/24 vs 16/24.\n    No with_skill page moved. research/17-tailwind-vs-css.md has the method.\n    \n    LA8 down-weighted 3 to 1. It was verified from practitioner claims; the\n    side-tab measurement found 2 of 12. The register's own rule applied. It\n    also gains the shadcn spelling the corpus uses and the CSS form.\n    \n    anti-antropik-design vendored verbatim under vendor/ as the fallback brand\n    guard. The installed copy always wins; the vendored one never competes on\n    mtime; the proof line now says (installed) or (vendored); a selftest pins\n    the vendored fingerprint at 5697117fa1b27195. Fail-closed is intact:\n    ANTI_ANTROPIK_NO_VENDORED=1 reproduces NOT RUN and exit 1, and stop_check's\n    selftest uses it. No licence file exists in the source; vendor/README.md\n=== message ===\nAudit fixes, batch 1: ten CSS twins, the sibling vendored, and the small ones\n\nThe end-to-end audit (workspace/AUDIT-REPORT.md) found that ten of thirty\ncode rules only recognised the Tailwind spelling of their tell. A plain-CSS\npage carrying the same tell scored 0. Fixed test-first: a CSS-form page\nthat all ten must fire on (watched it fail naming all ten), one twin per\nrule holding the rule's own threshold, and a well-made plain-CSS page that\nnone may fire on. One twin came out too loose - TY4 fired at -0.01em where\nTailwind's tracking-tight is -0.025em - and was set to the Tailwind value.\nThat is matching the definition, not weakening to pass.\n\nWhat moved on real pages: the baseline clinic page 0 to 2 (the eyebrows\nthat started this), budget baseline 0 to 4, deslop baseline 18 to 20 and\nnow FAIL. Iteration 2 regraded by the grader, not by hand: 24/24 vs 16/24.\nNo with_skill page moved. research/17-tailwind-vs-css.md has the method.\n\nLA8 down-weighted 3 to 1. It was verified from practitioner claims; the\nside-tab measurement found 2 of 12. The register's own rule applied. It\nalso gains the shadcn spelling the corpus uses and the CSS form.\n\nanti-antropik-design vendored verbatim under vendor/ as the fallback brand\nguard. The installed copy always wins; the vendored one never competes on\nmtime; the proof line now says (installed) or (vendored); a selftest pins\nthe vendored fingerprint at 5697117fa1b27195. Fail-closed is intact:\nANTI_ANTROPIK_NO_VENDORED=1 reproduces NOT RUN and exit 1, and stop_check's\nselftest uses it. No licence file exists in the source; vendor/README.md\nsays so plainly instead of guessing.\n\nAlso: /design-fix now reads its file argument; hookify_check honours\nHOOKIFY_PATH; scripts are executable; .serena/ untracked; SKILL.md at 299\nlines with the long lessons moved to reference/lessons.md and the newest\nones folded in; the sub-validator's \"skill not shipped\" finding withdrawn\nbecause the docs allow a root SKILL.md and `claude plugin validate` passes.\n\nFingerprint b7cd873aa4831ab9 -> 6b7abae241e662e5. Ten selftests PASS, 150\ncitations resolve, package 0.72 MB.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"585f6359-73cb-41ce-9216-ca134bcf08f7","timestamp":"2026-09-11T10:56:59.747Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0c3aed07-f958-4617-b719-48792f523d5d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ben/Downloads/Repos/Anti-AI-design skill","sessionId":"197143f0-7529-44af-b92c-d0b52a1bc034","version":"2.1.266","gitBranch":"main","slug":"users-ben-downloads-repos-anti-ai-desig-wild-shamir"}

{"parentUuid":"60feb20f-cb38-4481-ac62-cc04e8c32f3c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"28c9635e-ade4-4ae1-9a22-da089274ff53","agentId":"a47354933b4394c87","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016FSrnMw8fm7AMge9suDWNA","type":"tool_result","content":"    return bool(path) and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(path, MARKER))\n\n\nVENDORED = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))),\n                        \"vendor\", NAME)\n\n\ndef source_of(path):\n    \"\"\"'vendored' when the guard came from this skill's own copy, else 'installed'.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return \"vendored\" if os.path.realpath(path) == os.path.realpath(VENDORED) else \"installed\"\n    except Exception:\n        return \"installed\"\n\n\ndef candidates(home=None, env=None, allow_vendored=True):\n    \"\"\"Every place worth looking, in priority order.\n\n    Env var first so anyone can override. Then the plain skills folder, which\n    is stable. Plugin and session-cache paths come last and are globbed,\n    because their middle segments are generated ids.\n    \"\"\"\n    home = home or os.path.expanduser(\"~\")\n    env = os.environ if env is None else env\n    out = []\n    override = env.get(\"ANTI_ANTROPIK_PATH\")\n    if override:\n        out.append(override)\n    out.append(os.path.join(home, \".claude\", \"skills\", NAME))\n    out.extend(sorted(glob.glob(\n        os.path.join(home, \".claude\", \"plugins\", \"**\", NAME), recursive=True)))\n    out.extend(sorted(glob.glob(\n        os.path.join(home, \"Library\", \"Application Support\", \"Claude\",\n                     \"**\", \"skills\", NAME), recursive=True)))\n    # The copy vendored inside this skill comes last. It never competes on\n    # modification time with an installed copy: a freshly copied folder is\n    # always the newest, and it must never win on that basis.\n    # ANTI_ANTROPIK_NO_VENDORED=1 switches the fallback off. Tests that must\n    # see the fail-closed path use it, and it crosses subprocess boundaries\n    # where a function argument cannot.\n    if allow_vendored and not env.get(\"ANTI_ANTROPIK_NO_VENDORED\"):\n        out.append(VENDORED)\n    return out\n\n\ndef locate(home=None, env=None, allow_vendored=True):\n    \"\"\"Return the path to the brand guard, or None.\n\n    Among globbed hits, newest wins: a session cache can hold several copies\n    and the most recently written one is the one the running session uses.\n    \"\"\"\n    found = [p for p in candidates(home, env, allow_vendored) if _usable(p)]\n    if not found:\n        return None\n    override = (os.environ if env is None else env).get(\"ANTI_ANTROPIK_PATH\")\n    if override and found[0] == override:\n        return override\n    installed = [p for p in found if source_of(p) == \"installed\"]\n    if installed:\n        return max(installed, key=lambda p: os.path.getmtime(p))\n    return found[-1]  # the vendored copy, only when nothing else exists\n\n\ndef selftest():\n    \"\"\"Known-answer tests, including the case where nothing is installed.\"\"\"\n    checks = []","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"2e4ad988-bf04-4da5-93c3-1a1342922f13","timestamp":"2026-09-11T10:59:13.304Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"60feb20f-cb38-4481-ac62-cc04e8c32f3c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ben/Downloads/Repos/Anti-AI-design skill","sessionId":"197143f0-7529-44af-b92c-d0b52a1bc034","version":"2.1.266","gitBranch":"main","slug":"users-ben-downloads-repos-anti-ai-desig-wild-shamir"}

{"parentUuid":"9a5fc294-6f2f-4dc4-a1a9-cd799b1eaea1","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a47354933b4394c87","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CewY1etH1QXgEaPm5zKWZ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I reviewed the diff, read the surrounding files, and ran the scanners against hand-built probe pages. Findings below are all empirically reproduced.\n\n## Findings\n\n### 1. [Critical] The CO3 CSS twin misses the canonical AI gradient — hex-only, and `[^)]*` cannot cross a nested `rgba(...)`\n**Location**: `anti-ai-design-style/scripts/rules.json`, CO3 pattern `linear-gradient\\([^)]*#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,6}\\b[^)]*#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,6}\\b[^)]*\\)`\n**Finding**: The twin only fires when both colour stops are hex literals. Every gradient whose stops are `rgb()`, `rgba()`, `hsl()`, or CSS colour names scores 0 — and for the function forms it is structurally impossible to match, because `[^)]*` stops at the first `)`, which is the colour function's own closing paren. `rgba()` stops are the single most common spelling emitted by hand-coded and Framer/Webflow output.\n**Evidence**: `/tmp/probe/slopcss.html` with five textbook purple/pink/orange gradients:\n```\n.a{background:linear-gradient(135deg,rgba(139,92,246,1) 0%,rgba(236,72,153,1) 100%)}\n.b{background:linear-gradient(90deg,rgb(59,130,246),rgb(168,85,247))}\n.c{background:linear-gradient(to right,hsl(20,90%,55%),hsl(0,84%,60%))}\n.d{background:linear-gradient(120deg,cyan,indigo)}\n.e{background:linear-gradient(45deg,rgba(244,63,94,.9),rgba(251,146,60,.9))}\n```\n`ai_tell_scan.py` reports CO3 **not fired**, total AI-look 4/100. `CSS_FORM_FIXTURE` uses only hex stops, so the red fixture cannot see this.\n**Suggestion**: Match the stop count rather than the literal form, e.g. `linear-gradient\\((?:[^()]|\\([^()]*\\))*?(?:#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}|rgba?\\(|hsla?\\(|\\b(?:red|blue|purple|pink|cyan|indigo|violet|teal|orange|fuchsia|emerald)\\b)(?:[^()]|\\([^()]*\\))*?(?:#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}|rgba?\\(|hsla?\\()`, and add an rgba/hsl gradient to `CSS_FORM_FIXTURE` so the gap stays closed.\n\n### 2. [Critical] CO3's CSS twin is simultaneously looser than the Tailwind rule it mirrors: it counts monochrome gradients\n**Location**: `rules.json`, CO3 (rule name: \"Many two-hue gradient washes\")\n**Finding**: The Tailwind form requires `from-<hue>-N00 … to-<hue>-N00`, i.e. named palette hues. The CSS form matches any two hex stops, including grey-to-grey and same-hue shading, which is exactly the restrained usage the rule is supposed to permit.\n**Evidence**: `/tmp/probe/honest.html` — six neutral `linear-gradient(to bottom,#fafafa,#f0f0f0)`-style panel shades — fires CO3 for +4 and names three grey gradients as evidence. `CSS_CLEAN_FIXTURE` contains exactly one gradient, below `min_distinct: 5`, so the clean guard cannot catch this.\n**Suggestion**: Require the two stops to differ meaningfully in hue, or at minimum exclude stop pairs whose R/G/B channels are within a few points of each other. Add a multi-gradient neutral page to `CSS_CLEAN_FIXTURE`.\n\n### 3. [Warning] CO4 and LA9 CSS twins are case-sensitive on hex literals\n**Location**: `rules.json` CO4 combo item 1 (`#(?:0f172a|111827|18181b|09090b|020617|000000|000)`) and LA9 (`#(?:e5e7eb|e2e8f0|e4e4e7|d1d5db)`); `ai_tell_scan.py:98-105` `compile_patterns` compiles with no flags\n**Finding**: CO3 uses `[0-9a-fA-F]`; these two hard-code lowercase. `#0F172A` and `#E5E7EB` — the spelling most editors and design tools emit — are invisible.\n**Evidence**: In `/tmp/probe/slopcss.html`, `.dark{background:#0F172A}` did not satisfy CO4's dark-background arm; CO4 only fired because the other three arms hit `combo_min: 3`. `.card{border:1px solid #E5E7EB;box-shadow:0 20px 25px -5px …}` did not fire LA9 at all. Lowercasing both makes LA9 fire.\n**Suggestion**: Use `(?i:…)` on the hex alternations, or spell them `[eE]5[eE]7[eE][bB]`-style. The clean fixture uses lowercase everywhere, so it cannot surface this.\n\n### 4. [Warning] The shipped-placeholder check flags any bracketed acronym, and it flips the file to FAIL\n**Location**: `copy_check.py`, `check_text`, placeholder regex branch `\\[\\s*(?:[A-Z][A-Z ]{2,40}|…)`\n**Finding**: `[A-Z][A-Z ]{2,40}` matches any all-caps run of three or more characters inside square brackets. Ordinary copy containing bracketed acronyms, carrier names, format labels or cross-reference markers is reported as a shipped blank, and since a placeholder finding is non-empty the verdict becomes `FIX AND RE-CHECK`.\n**Evidence**:\n```\n$ python3 copy_check.py /tmp/probe/copy.html\n  - Shipped placeholder(s): [DHL], [VISA], [PDF MENU]\nVerdict: FIX AND RE-CHECK\n```\n`BRACKETS_OK_COPY` contains only `[1]`, `[Enter]`, `[optional]` and `[read more]` — no all-caps acronym — so the guard fixture is blind to the entire first alternation.\n**Suggestion**: Require the all-caps run to contain a whitespace-separated field word (`NAME|ADDRESS|PHONE|EMAIL|CITY|PRICE|DATE|COMPANY|LOGO|TBD|XXX`) rather than accepting any capitals, and add `[DHL] [VISA] [PDF MENU]` to `BRACKETS_OK_COPY`.\n\n### 5. [Warning] CR8 treats a page-wide `requires` as block scope, so one inverted logo reads as \"dark mode by inversion\"\n**Location**: `rules.json` CR8; `ai_tell_scan.py:238-256` (`has_req = any(re.search(r, all_text) …)`)\n**Finding**: `requires: prefers-color-scheme: dark` is tested against the whole concatenated text, not against the block containing `filter: invert(1)`. A page with a properly token-driven dark-mode block plus any unrelated inversion — the standard trick for a single-colour logo mark — is reported as having flipped its theme.\n**Evidence**: `/tmp/probe/cr8.html` defines dark tokens inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` and has one `.logo-mark{filter:invert(1)}`. Output: `[CR8] Dark mode by inversion`. `CSS_CLEAN_FIXTURE` has no `prefers-color-scheme` block at all, so the clean guard never exercises this path.\n**Suggestion**: Scope the match — require `invert(` to appear inside the `prefers-color-scheme: dark` block (match the block first, then search within it), or exempt `filter: invert` declarations whose selector mentions logo/icon/mark.\n\n### 6. [Warning] `--codex` installs 121 files that `--uninstall` cannot remove, and its dry run claims a write it did not make\n**Location**: `install.py`, `install_codex` and `main`\n**Finding**: Two problems on the same path. (a) `uninstall` only knows the `.claude` layout; after a Codex install it reports success while leaving the tree in place. (b) The AGENTS.md line is printed in the past tense unconditionally, outside the `if not dry_run` guard.\n**Evidence**:\n```\n$ python3 install.py /tmp/probe/codex --codex\nCopied 121 files into …/.agents/skills/anti-ai-design-style\n$ du -sh /tmp/probe/codex          → 2.2M\n$ python3 install.py /tmp/probe/codex --uninstall --dry-run\nNothing to remove: no files from this skill in /tmp/probe/codex/.claude\n```\nand on `--codex --dry-run` (which writes nothing): `Also added a short 'Design checks' section to /tmp/probe/codex/AGENTS.md (created it if missing).` Note also that `install.py`'s `selftest()` covers only the Claude layout — the Codex path ships with no fixture at all, against the change's stated fixture-first rule.\n**Suggestion**: Teach `uninstall` the `.agents/skills/<name>` tree (and the AGENTS.md section); move the AGENTS.md print inside the guard or phrase it \"Would add\"; add a selftest that installs to a temp dir, asserts the tree exists, uninstalls, and asserts it is gone.\n\n### 7. [Warning] The vendored-fingerprint check in `find_brand_guard --selftest` is appended after the results are printed, so a failure is invisible\n**Location**: `find_brand_guard.py`, `selftest()` — `checks.append((\"vendored copy reports fingerprint …\"))` sits below the `for label, ok in checks: print(...)` loop\n**Finding**: The check contributes to `passed` but its label is never printed. If the vendored copy drifts, or the `subprocess.run` raises (making `fp = \"error: …\"`), the operator sees a bare `SELFTEST: FAIL` with every printed line reading `ok`, and no indication of which check failed.\n**Evidence**: Current run prints five `ok` lines then `SELFTEST: PASS`; the fingerprint line is absent from the output entirely. The hard-coded `5697117fa1b27195` is also a silent tripwire.\n**Suggestion**: Compute the fingerprint check before the print loop. Also print the expected-vs-got fingerprint on failure.\n\n### 8. [Warning] `--allow-network` changes what was measured but leaves no trace in the proof line or the JSON, and is a silent no-op without `--render`\n**Location**: `verify_all.py`, `render_result` / `gather` / `summarise` / `proof_line`\n**Finding**: The flag's own help says \"use for live sites, never for the repeatable local check\", yet nothing downstream records it: `render_result` returns the same `{\"state\", \"detail\"}` shape either way and `summarise` never sees the flag. Two proof lines — one hermetic, one that loaded whatever CDNs and fonts the page referenced — are byte-identical. Separately, `render_result` returns `SKIPPED` at `if not enabled`, so `verify_all.py --allow-network page.html` (without `--render`) silently does nothing while implying a live audit ran.\n**Evidence**: `grep -n \"allow_network\" verify_all.py` shows the flag reaching only the subprocess argv (lines 78-79); `summary[\"render\"]` carries `state`/`detail`/`aa_failures` only, and `proof_line` renders `rendered {state}`.\n**Suggestion**: Put `allow_network` into the render dict and render it as `rendered PASS (network)`; and warn on stderr when `--allow-network` is passed without `--render`.\n\n### 9. [Nit] TY4's CSS font-size arm only covers 40–99px, and several twins match across digit boundaries\n**Location**: `rules.json` TY4 (`[4-9]\\dpx`, `[3-9](?:\\.\\d+)?rem`), LA3 (`(?:8|10|12|16)px`)\n**Finding**: None of these alternations are bounded. `[4-9]\\dpx` cannot match a three-digit hero size, so `font-size:120px` — larger than anything Tailwind's `text-7xl` produces — is missed, while the same literal would match if it read `96px`. In the other direction the unbounded forms can match inside a longer number.\n**Evidence**: `/tmp/probe/honest.html` has `font-size:140px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:-.03em`, a full three-of-three hero sandwich; TY4 does not fire. Re-run with `96px` and it does.\n**Suggestion**: `(?<!\\d)(?:[4-9]\\d|[1-9]\\d\\d)px\\b` and add a word boundary to the rem and radius alternations.\n\n**Not findings, checked and clear**: the `requires`-gate change preserves CR2 (the only pre-existing `requires` rule, which has no `patterns` and still takes the `chk.get(\"missing\", [])` path — verified firing on `/tmp/probe/honest.html`); `missing` is honoured for CR9 via the count path at `ai_tell_scan.py:265-267`; fail-closed survives (`ANTI_ANTROPIK_NO_VENDORED=1` with an empty HOME gives `brand distance NOT RUN` and exit 1); the vendored copy loses to an installed one and the `(installed)`/`(vendored)` tag is correct, including when the vendored tree is itself reached through a plugin glob, because `source_of` compares realpaths;
